| 162 | Deadlines and filing | \| Pensions from Germany \| A non-resident whose only German income is pensions under § 49(1) nos. 7 and 10: Finanzamt Neubrandenburg, also where such a person applies under § 1(3) (§ 19(6) AO; § 1 EStZustV) \| | AO §19(6) https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/ao_1977/__19.html<br>EStZustV §1 https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/estzustv/__1.html | (6) Das Bundesministerium der Finanzen kann zur Sicherstellung der Besteuerung von Personen, die nach § 1 Absatz 4 des Einkommensteuergesetzes beschränkt steuerpflichtig sind und Einkünfte im Sinne von § 49 Absatz 1 Nummer 7 und 10 des Einkommensteuergesetzes beziehen, durch Rechtsverordnung mit Zustimmung des Bundesrates einer Finanzbehörde die örtliche Zuständigkeit für den Geltungsbereich des Gesetzes übertragen. Satz 1 gilt auch in den Fällen, in denen ein Antrag nach § 1 Absatz 3 des Einkommensteuergesetzes gestellt wird.<br>Für die Besteuerung nach dem Einkommen von Personen, die nach § 1 Abs. 4 des Einkommensteuergesetzes beschränkt einkommensteuerpflichtig sind und ausschließlich mit Einkünften im Sinne des § 49 Abs. 1 Nr. 7 und 10 des Einkommensteuergesetzes zu veranlagen sind, ist das Finanzamt Neubrandenburg örtlich zuständig. Das Finanzamt Neubrandenburg ist ebenfalls zuständig in den Fällen des § 19 Abs. 6 Satz 2 der Abgabenordnung. | 2026-10-29 |
| 163 | Deadlines and filing | \| Own return, 2026 \| 31 July 2027, a Saturday, so Monday 2 August 2027 (§ 149(2), § 108(3) AO) \| | AO149(2) https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/ao_1977/__149.html<br>AO §108(3) https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/ao_1977/__108.html | (2) Soweit die Steuergesetze nichts anderes bestimmen, sind Steuererklärungen, die sich auf ein Kalenderjahr oder auf einen gesetzlich bestimmten Zeitpunkt beziehen, spätestens sieben Monate nach Ablauf des Kalenderjahres oder sieben Monate nach dem gesetzlich bestimmten Zeitpunkt abzugeben. Bei Steuerpflichtigen, die den Gewinn aus Land- und Forstwirtschaft nach einem vom Kalenderjahr abweichenden Wirtschaftsjahr ermitteln, endet die Frist nicht vor Ablauf des siebten Monats, der auf den Schluss des in dem Kalenderjahr begonnenen Wirtschaftsjahres folgt.<br>(3) Fällt das Ende einer Frist auf einen Sonntag, einen gesetzlichen Feiertag oder einen Sonnabend, so endet die Frist mit dem Ablauf des nächstfolgenden Werktags. | 2026-10-29 |

Juli des zweiten auf den Besteuerungszeitraum folgenden Kalenderjahres abzugeben. | 2026-10-29 |
| 165 | Deadlines and filing | \| § 50a withholding return (payer) \| By the 10th of the month after each calendar quarter, to the BZSt (§ 50a(5)) \| | EStG §50a(5) https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/estg/__50a.html | (5) 1Die Steuer entsteht in dem Zeitpunkt, in dem die Vergütung dem Gläubiger zufließt. 2In diesem Zeitpunkt hat der Schuldner der Vergütung den Steuerabzug für Rechnung des Gläubigers (Steuerschuldner) vorzunehmen. 3Er hat die innerhalb eines Kalendervierteljahres einzubehaltende Steuer jeweils bis zum zehnten des dem Kalendervierteljahr folgenden Monats beim Bundeszentralamt für Steuern anzumelden und die einbehaltene Steuer an das Bundeszentralamt für Steuern abzuführen. 4Eine Anmeldungsverpflichtung beim Bundeszentralamt für Steuern besteht auch, wenn ein Steuerabzug auf Grund des Absatzes 2 Satz 3 oder des Absatzes 4 Satz 1 nicht vorzunehmen ist oder auf Grund des § 50c Absatz 2 nicht oder nicht in voller Höhe vorzunehmen ist; Satz 3 gilt insoweit entsprechend. 5Der Schuldner der Vergütung haftet für die Einbehaltung und Abführung der Steuer. 6Der Steuerschuldner kann in Anspruch genommen werden, wenn der Schuldner der Vergütung den Steuerabzug nicht vorschriftsmäßig vorgenommen hat. 7Der Schuldner der Vergütung ist verpflichtet, dem Gläubiger auf Verlangen die folgenden Angaben nach amtlich vorgeschriebenem Muster zu bescheinigen: 1.<br>den Namen und die Anschrift des Gläubigers,<br>2.<br>die Art der Tätigkeit und Höhe der Vergütung in Euro,<br>3.<br>den Zahlungstag,<br>4.<br>den Betrag der einbehaltenen und abgeführten Steuer nach Absatz 2 oder Absatz 3. | 2026-10-29 |
| 166 | Deadlines and filing | \| § 50c refund \| Four years from the end of the year the payment was received (§ 50c(3)) \| | EStG §50c(3) https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/estg/__50c.html | (3) 1Dem beschränkt steuerpflichtigen Gläubiger der Kapitalerträge oder Vergütungen wird auf seinen fristgemäßen Antrag beim Bundeszentralamt für Steuern (Erstattungsantrag) auf der Grundlage eines Freistellungsbescheides die gemäß Absatz 1 Satz 1 einbehaltene und abgeführte oder auf Grund eines Haftungsbescheids oder Nachforderungsbescheids entrichtete Steuer erstattet, wenn die Steuer nicht nach § 36 Absatz 2 Nummer 2 auf die Einkommensteuer oder die Körperschaftsteuer des Gläubigers angerechnet werden kann. 2Die Frist für einen Erstattungsantrag beträgt vier Jahre und beginnt mit Ablauf des Kalenderjahres, in dem die Kapitalerträge oder Vergütungen bezogen worden sind; sie endet nicht vor Ablauf eines Jahres seit dem Zeitpunkt der Entrichtung der Steuer und nicht vor Ablauf der im Abkommen zur Vermeidung der Doppelbesteuerung vorgesehenen Frist. 3Ein Freistellungsbescheid für Kapitalertragsteuer wird nur erteilt, wenn die in § 45a Absatz 2 oder Absatz 3 bezeichnete Bescheinigung vorgelegt wurde oder die Angaben gemäß § 45a Absatz 2a übermittelt wurden; einem Antrag auf Erstattung der nach § 50a entrichteten Steuer ist die Bescheinigung nach § 50a Absatz 5 Satz 7 beizufügen. 4Hat der Gläubiger nach § 50a Absatz 5 Steuern für Rechnung anderer beschränkt steuerpflichtiger Gläubiger einzubehalten, kann die Auszahlung des Erstattungsanspruchs davon abhängig gemacht werden, dass er die Zahlung der von ihm einzubehaltenden Steuer nachweist, hierfür Sicherheit leistet oder unwiderruflich die Zustimmung zur Verrechnung seines Erstattungsanspruchs mit dem Steueranspruch nach § 50a Absatz 5 Satz 3 erklärt. | 2026-10-29 |
| 167 | Deadlines and filing | \| Electronic filing \| Compulsory where there is business or self-employed income (§ 25(4) EStG) \| | EStG §25(4) https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/estg/__25.html | (4) 1Die Erklärung nach Absatz 3 ist nach amtlich vorgeschriebenem Datensatz durch Datenfernübertragung zu übermitteln, wenn Einkünfte nach § 2 Absatz 1 Satz 1 Nummer 1 bis 3 erzielt werden und es sich nicht um einen der Veranlagungsfälle gemäß § 46 Absatz 2 Nummer 2 bis 8 handelt. 2Auf Antrag kann die Finanzbehörde zur Vermeidung unbilliger Härten auf eine Übermittlung durch Datenfernübertragung verzichten. | 2026-10-29 |

nach dem Besteuerungszeitpunkt,<br>2.<br>in den Fällen des § 149 Absatz 2 Satz 2 nicht binnen 19 Monaten nach Ablauf des Kalenderjahrs oder nicht binnen 19 Monaten nach dem Besteuerungszeitpunkt oder<br>3.<br>in den Fällen des § 149 Absatz 4 nicht bis zu dem in der Anordnung bestimmten Zeitpunkt<br>abgegeben wurde.<br>(3) Absatz 2 gilt nicht, 1.<br>wenn die Finanzbehörde die Frist für die Abgabe der Steuererklärung nach § 109 verlängert hat oder diese Frist rückwirkend verlängert,<br>2.<br>wenn die Steuer auf null Euro oder auf einen negativen Betrag festgesetzt wird,<br>3.<br>wenn die festgesetzte Steuer die Summe der festgesetzten Vorauszahlungen und der anzurechnenden Steuerabzugsbeträge nicht übersteigt oder<br>4.<br>bei jährlich abzugebenden Lohnsteueranmeldungen, bei Anmeldungen von Umsatzsteuer-Sondervorauszahlungen nach § 48 Absatz 2 der Umsatzsteuer-Durchführungsverordnung, bei jährlich abzugebenden Versicherungsteuer- und Feuerschutzsteueranmeldungen sowie bei Erklärungen nach § 95 des Mindeststeuergesetzes.<br>(5) Der Verspätungszuschlag beträgt vorbehaltlich des Satzes 2, der Absätze 8 und 13 Satz 2 für jeden angefangenen Monat der eingetretenen Wurde ein Erklärungspflichtiger von der Finanzbehörde erstmals nach Ablauf der gesetzlichen Erklärungsfrist zur Abgabe einer Steuererklärung innerhalb einer dort bezeichneten Frist aufgefordert und konnte er bis zum Zugang dieser Aufforderung davon ausgehen, keine Steuererklärung abgeben zu müssen, so ist der Verspätungszuschlag nur für die Monate zu berechnen, die nach dem Ablauf der in der Aufforderung bezeichneten Erklärungsfrist begonnen haben.<br>(10) Der Verspätungszuschlag ist auf volle Euro abzurunden und darf höchstens 25 000 Euro betragen. | 2026-10-29 |

Das Gleiche gilt für zurückzuzahlende Steuervergütungen und Haftungsschulden, soweit sich die Haftung auf Steuern und zurückzuzahlende Steuervergütungen erstreckt. Die Säumnis nach Satz 1 tritt nicht ein, bevor die Steuer festgesetzt oder angemeldet worden ist. Wird die Festsetzung einer Steuer oder Steuervergütung aufgehoben, geändert oder nach § 129 berichtigt, so bleiben die bis dahin verwirkten Säumniszuschläge unberührt; das Gleiche gilt, wenn ein Haftungsbescheid zurückgenommen, widerrufen oder nach § 129 berichtigt wird. Erlischt der Anspruch durch Aufrechnung, bleiben Säumniszuschläge unberührt, die bis zur Fälligkeit der Schuld des Aufrechnenden entstanden sind. | 2026-10-29 |

| 178 | Cross-border points | - **Treaties override.** A treaty can take away Germany's right to tax (for example employment income under the 183-day rule) or cap it (dividends, royalties). Withholding still happens first; relief is by exemption certificate or refund (§ 50c). | EStG §50c(1)/(2) https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/estg/__50c.html | (1) 1Soweit der Besteuerung von Einkünften, die der Kapitalertragsteuer oder dem Steuerabzug nach § 50a unterliegen, der § 43b, der § 50g oder ein Abkommen zur Vermeidung der Doppelbesteuerung entgegenstehen, sind dessen ungeachtet die Vorschriften zur Einbehaltung, Abführung und Anmeldung der Steuer anzuwenden. 2Der zum Steuerabzug Verpflichtete kann sich vorbehaltlich des Absatzes 2 nicht auf die Rechte des Gläubigers der Kapitalerträge oder Vergütungen aus § 43b, § 50g oder dem Abkommen berufen.<br>(2) 1Der Schuldner der Kapitalerträge oder Vergütungen ist zur Einbehaltung und Abführung der Steuer nicht verpflichtet, 1.<br>soweit dem Gläubiger der Kapitalerträge oder Vergütungen auf dessen Antrag (Freistellungsantrag) vom Bundeszentralamt für Steuern bescheinigt wird, dass § 43b, § 50g oder ein Abkommen zur Vermeidung der Doppelbesteuerung der Besteuerung der Einkünfte entgegensteht (Freistellungsbescheinigung), oder<br>2.<br>soweit es sich um Einkünfte eines beschränkt Steuerpflichtigen im Sinne des § 50a Absatz 1 Nummer 3 handelt, der Besteuerung der Einkünfte ein Abkommen zur Vermeidung der Doppelbesteuerung entgegensteht und durch die Vergütung zuzüglich der dem beschränkt Steuerpflichtigen in demselben Kalenderjahr vom Schuldner bereits zugeflossenen Vergütungen 10 000 Euro nicht überschritten werden.<br>2Der Schuldner ist zur Steueranmeldung auch dann verpflichtet, wenn er gemäß Satz 1 keine Steuer einzubehalten und abzuführen hat. 3Eine Steueranmeldung kann auf der Grundlage des Satzes 1 nicht geändert werden, es sei denn, die Freistellungsbescheinigung ist zum Zeitpunkt der Anmeldung der Steuer noch nicht erteilt worden. 4Eine Freistellungsbescheinigung ist auf einen Zeitraum von höchstens fünf Jahren frühestens ab dem Tag, an dem der Antrag beim Bundeszentralamt für Steuern eingeht, zu befristen und von der Einhaltung der Voraussetzungen ihrer Erteilung während ihrer Geltung abhängig zu machen; sie kann mit weiteren Nebenbestimmungen gemäß § 120 Absatz 2 der Abgabenordnung versehen werden. 5Eine Freistellungsbescheinigung für die Kapitalertragsteuer auf Grund eines Abkommens zur Vermeidung der Doppelbesteuerung ist nur zu erteilen, wenn der Gläubiger der Kapitalerträge eine Kapitalgesellschaft ist, die im Staat ihrer Ansässigkeit den Steuern vom Einkommen oder Gewinn unterliegt, ohne davon befreit zu sein, und soweit dem Gläubiger Kapitalerträge von einer unbeschränkt steuerpflichtigen Kapitalgesellschaft im Sinne des § 1 Absatz 1 Nummer 1 des Körperschaftsteuergesetzes zufließen, an deren Nennkapital der Gläubiger zu mindestens einem Zehntel unmittelbar beteiligt ist. 6Über einen Freistellungsantrag ist innerhalb von drei Monaten nach Vorlage aller erforderlichen Nachweise zu entscheiden.<br>Exact evidence limit: The183-day statement is a generic treaty illustration/referral, not an established result under an identified treaty. Full employer/PE and reference-period conditions are not supplied here. | 2026-10-29 |

| 192 | Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat | **§ 1(3) option.** German income is 24,000 / 26,000 = 92.3% of her total, and the Austrian income is under EUR 12,348 (Austria is in country group 1), so both tests pass. She needs a certificate from the Austrian tax office for the EUR 2,000. Taxable income 24,000 − 36 = EUR 23,964. The Austrian rent is not subject to German tax, so it raises the rate (§ 32b(1) no. 5; the EU/EEA rent exception in § 32b(1) sentence 2 covers only no. 3): tax on 23,964 + 2,000 = 25,964 is EUR 3,107, a rate of 11.9666%. Tax = 23,964 × 11.9666% = EUR 2,867. | 237 | Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad | If he had chosen the tariff under § 32d(6), § 32d(5) still governs the credit, applied to the additional tariff tax on the capital income. The foreign tax must first be reduced by any source-country refund entitlement. If 30% (EUR 3,000) were withheld but the same treaty limits source tax to 15%, only EUR 1,500 is creditable in this case and EUR 1,500 is reclaimed from the source country; the German tax cap does not turn treaty-refundable tax into a German credit. | 266 | Ask the client first | 1. Where do you live, and do you keep any home in Germany you can use? Did you stay in Germany more than six months in a row? This decides full or limited liability. | — | intake question; actual amounts/gates sourced in current rule rows | — |
| 267 | Ask the client first | 2. What German income do you have (wages for work done in Germany, rent, a business, director or board fees, performances, royalties, dividends), and was tax withheld from it? | — | intake question; actual amounts/gates sourced in current rule rows | — |
| 268 | Ask the client first | 3. What is your total income from all countries for 2026? This decides the § 1(3) test. | — | intake question; actual amounts/gates sourced in current rule rows | — |
| 269 | Ask the client first | 4. Your nationality, and where your spouse lives. EU/EEA nationals may get splitting under § 1a. | — | intake question; actual amounts/gates sourced in current rule rows | — |
| 270 | Ask the client first | 5. Which country taxes you as a resident, and do you have its residence certificate? Treaty refunds and the § 1(3) option need one. | — | intake question; actual amounts/gates sourced in current rule rows | — |
| 271 | Ask the client first | 6. For a resident with foreign income: what type of income, which country, how much tax was paid, and was any of it refundable there? | — | intake question; actual amounts/gates sourced in current rule rows | — |

| fb85812a814d:15 | This skill decides whether a person is taxed in Germany on worldwide income or only on German income, then works out how the German income is taxed: by assessment, by wage tax, or by a flat withholding. It also covers the reverse case: a German resident with foreign income who wants credit for the foreign tax. | Exact wording retained at current LF18, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| fb85812a814d:17 | See also, and do not repeat here: | Exact wording retained at current LF20, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| fb85812a814d:19 | - **de-employee-income-tax**: the 2026 tariff, Werbungskosten and special expenses for a resident employee. The tariff formula there is the one used below. | Exact wording retained at current LF22, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| fb85812a814d:20 | - **de-rental-income**: how to compute the rental result (AfA, Werbungskosten, Anlage V). A non-resident landlord computes it the same way. | Exact wording retained at current LF23, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| fb85812a814d:21 | - **de-capital-gains**: the flat tax on investment income, the Sparer-Pauschbetrag and the loss pots. | Exact wording retained at current LF24, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| fb85812a814d:22 | - **de-payroll** and **germany-payroll**: running wage tax for an employee, including tax class I. | Exact wording retained at current LF25, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| fb85812a814d:23 | - **de-corporate-tax**: corporate tax at 15%, also for the German permanent establishment of a foreign company. | Exact wording retained at current LF26, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| fb85812a814d:24 | - **leaving-germany-exit-tax**: shareholders who move out, and extended limited liability after a move to a low-tax country (§ 2 AStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF27, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| fb85812a814d:25 | - **germany-to-switzerland-cross-border-tax**: Swiss commuters and moves to Switzerland (still on 2025 figures). | Swiss commuter handoff retained at current LF29; actual refreshed corridor Guide replaces the historical missing-method warning. |
| fb85812a814d:29 | **Unlimited liability (§ 1(1) EStG).** A person with a residence (Wohnsitz) or habitual abode (gewöhnlicher Aufenthalt) in Germany is taxed on worldwide income. | Exact wording retained at current LF33, Who this applies to |
| fb85812a814d:31 | - **Residence (§ 8 AO):** a home the person keeps and uses, in circumstances showing they will keep and use it. A flat kept available in Germany is enough, even if the person spends most of the year abroad. | Exact wording retained at current LF35, Who this applies to |
| fb85812a814d:32 | - **Habitual abode (§ 9 AO):** staying in a way that shows the stay is not just temporary. A continuous stay of more than six months always counts, from the first day; short breaks are ignored. The six-month rule does not apply to a purely private stay (visit, holiday, cure) of up to one year (§ 9 sentence 3 AO). | Exact wording retained at current LF36, Who this applies to |
| fb85812a814d:33 | - **German state employees abroad (§ 1(2) EStG):** German nationals paid from a German public purse, taxed abroad only like non-residents, stay fully liable. | Exact wording retained at current LF37, Who this applies to |
| fb85812a814d:35 | **Limited liability (§ 1(4) EStG).** A person with neither residence nor habitual abode in Germany is taxed only on German-source income listed in § 49 EStG. | Exact wording retained at current LF39, Who this applies to |
| fb85812a814d:37 | **The § 1(3) option.** A non-resident can apply to be treated as fully liable for their German income if, in the calendar year, either: | Exact wording retained at current LF41, Who this applies to |
| fb85812a814d:39 | - at least 90% of their total income is subject to German income tax, or | Exact wording retained at current LF43, Who this applies to |
| fb85812a814d:40 | - the income not subject to German tax is no more than the Grundfreibetrag, EUR 12,348 for 2026. The amount is cut to ¾, ½ or ¼ for residents of lower-cost countries (BMF country groups; Austria and most of Western Europe are in group 1, full amount). | Exact wording retained at current LF44, Who this applies to |
| fb85812a814d:42 | German income that a tax treaty limits to a reduced rate (for example dividends) counts as not subject to German tax for the test (§ 1(3) sentence 3). The foreign income must be shown by a certificate from the foreign tax authority (§ 1(3) sentence 5). § 50a withholding still applies (§ 1(3) sentence 6). | Exact wording retained at current LF46, Who this applies to |
| fb85812a814d:44 | **§ 1a EStG, EU/EEA nationals.** An EU or EEA national who is fully liable under § 1(1) or treated so under § 1(3) can have a spouse who lives in another EU/EEA state treated as fully liable, so the couple gets joint assessment and splitting. For the § 1(3) test the income of both spouses counts and the Grundfreibetrag is doubled to EUR 24,696 (§ 1a(1) no. 2). A non-EU/EEA national under § 1(3) gets the Grundfreibetrag but not splitting. | Exact wording retained at current LF48, Who this applies to |
| fb85812a814d:46 | **Companies.** A company with neither its seat nor its place of management in Germany has limited corporate tax liability on its German income (§ 2 no. 1 KStG). Income settled by withholding is final unless it belongs to a German business (§ 32(1) KStG). A German permanent establishment is assessed at the 15% corporate rate (§ 23 KStG; see de-corporate-tax). | Exact wording retained at current LF50, Who this applies to |

| fb85812a814d:93 | Income that has had wage tax, Kapitalertragsteuer or § 50a tax withheld is settled by the withholding. No return is filed for it. Exceptions where an assessment follows: | Exact wording retained at current LF97, Step 2: is the tax settled by withholding? (§ 50(2) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:95 | - income of a German business (permanent establishment); | Exact wording retained at current LF99, Step 2: is the tax settled by withholding? (§ 50(2) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:96 | - employment income where an allowance was entered in the wage tax data and pay exceeds the Grundfreibetrag plus lump sums, or where the employee applies for an assessment (EU/EEA nationals resident in the EU/EEA, and in some cases Switzerland), or in the § 46(2) nos. 2, 5 and 5a cases; | Exact wording retained at current LF100, Step 2: is the tax settled by withholding? (§ 50(2) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:97 | - § 50a artists, athletes and supervisory board members who apply for an assessment (EU/EEA nationals resident in the EU/EEA; assessed by the BZSt); | Exact wording retained at current LF101, Step 2: is the tax settled by withholding? (§ 50(2) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:98 | - the person is assessed as fully liable under § 1(3) or § 1a. | Exact wording retained at current LF102, Step 2: is the tax settled by withholding? (§ 50(2) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:100 | Income with no withholding, such as rent, a German business or a sale of German land, is always assessed. | Exact wording retained at current LF104, Step 2: is the tax settled by withholding? (§ 50(2) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:104 | 1. **Income.** Compute each type as for a resident. Business expenses and Werbungskosten count only if they relate to the German income. | Exact wording retained at current LF108, Step 3: the assessment of a non-resident (§ 50(1) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:105 | 2. **No personal deductions.** Special expenses, the EUR 36 lump sum, single-parent relief, child allowances, extraordinary burdens and § 35a credits do not apply (§ 50(1) sentence 4). There is no splitting. | DE-L13 corrected general exclusion with own50(1a) professional-pension exception at current LF109. |
| fb85812a814d:106 | 3. **Employees are the exception.** For employment income under § 49(1) no. 4, pension and health insurance contributions and the EUR 36 lump sum are allowed, for the period of the German employment and up to that employment income (§ 50(1) sentence 5). The employee lump sum (EUR 1,230, § 9a) is a Werbungskosten lump sum and applies anyway; it and the EUR 36 are cut pro rata for part-year work (§ 50(1) sentence 6). | Exact wording retained at current LF110, Step 3: the assessment of a non-resident (§ 50(1) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:107 | 4. **Grundfreibetrag added back.** Apply the tariff to taxable income plus EUR 12,348. For employment income, add only the part of EUR 12,348 above the employment income less allowed deductions. In effect a non-resident employee keeps the allowance on wages; a landlord or business owner does not. | Exact wording retained at current LF111, Step 3: the assessment of a non-resident (§ 50(1) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:108 | 5. **Solidarity surcharge** at 5.5% of the income tax, with the same EUR 20,350 threshold as residents. | Exact wording retained at current LF112, Step 3: the assessment of a non-resident (§ 50(1) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:112 | The person is taxed as a resident on their German income: Grundfreibetrag, special expenses, the EUR 36 lump sum and the resident deductions apply. Income that is not subject to German tax is left out of the base but raises the rate (progression, § 32b(1) no. 5 EStG): the tax rate is found on taxable income plus that foreign income, then applied to taxable income alone. Splitting needs § 1a. Foreign capital income is a special case: § 2(5b) EStG keeps capital income under § 32d(1) out of "income" wherever the law refers to it, and whether foreign interest or dividends of a § 1(3) applicant count in the 90% test and in the progression is not settled here (see Not yet confirmed). | Exact wording retained at current LF116, Step 4: the § 1(3) assessment instead |
| fb85812a814d:116 | - Non-resident employees are in tax class I (§ 38b(1) no. 1(b) EStG), unless treated as fully liable under § 1(3) or § 1a. | Exact wording retained at current LF120, Wage tax for non-resident employees |
| fb85812a814d:117 | - The employer's Betriebsstättenfinanzamt (the tax office for the place of business), not a residence office, sets and changes their wage tax data and issues the tax ID (§ 39(2) sentence 2 and § 39(3) EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF121, Wage tax for non-resident employees |
| fb85812a814d:118 | - A § 1(3) or § 1a employee with wage tax data set this way must file (§ 46(2) no. 7(b) EStG). An employee who wants § 1(3) treatment only in the assessment applies with a return (§ 46(2) no. 9), at the employer's Betriebsstättenfinanzamt. | Exact wording retained at current LF122, Wage tax for non-resident employees |
| fb85812a814d:122 | - The German payer withholds when the payment is made: 15% of gross, 30% for supervisory board fees, plus 5.5% surcharge on the tax. Travel and hotel costs the payer refunds count as receipts only above actual cost and the per diem lump sums. | Exact wording retained at current LF126, § 50a withholding (artists, athletes, royalties, supervisory boards) |
| fb85812a814d:123 | - For an EU/EEA creditor the payer may deduct costs the creditor proves, then withhold 30% (individual) or 15% (company) of the net (§ 50a(3)). | DE-L12 corrected statutory net-route scope, current LF64/127; royalties no3 excluded, actual eligible nationals/residence/company and proven direct-cost conditions retained. |
| fb85812a814d:124 | - The payer files the withholding return with the BZSt by the 10th of the month after each calendar quarter and pays it over (§ 50a(5)). It must file even when nothing is withheld. The payer is liable for tax not withheld; the creditor can also be pursued. | Exact wording retained at current LF128, § 50a withholding (artists, athletes, royalties, supervisory boards) |
| fb85812a814d:125 | - On request the payer issues a certificate of the tax withheld (§ 50a(5) sentence 7). The creditor needs it for a refund. | Exact wording retained at current LF129, § 50a withholding (artists, athletes, royalties, supervisory boards) |
| fb85812a814d:129 | - The payer withholds in full even when a treaty limits German tax, unless the creditor holds an exemption certificate (Freistellungsbescheinigung) from the BZSt (§ 50c(1), (2)). For dividends, a treaty exemption certificate is given only to a company holding at least 10% of a German company. | Exact wording retained at current LF133, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:130 | - Small royalties: where a treaty bars German tax and the year's royalties from that payer stay at or below EUR 10,000, the payer need not withhold (§ 50c(2) no. 2), but must still file the withholding return. | Exact wording retained at current LF134, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:131 | - Refund: the creditor applies to the BZSt, electronically, within four years from the end of the year of receipt, with a residence certificate from their own tax authority and the payer's tax certificate (§ 50c(3), (5)). The BZSt warns that refunds can take more than 20 months. | Exact wording retained at current LF135, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:132 | - Companies: treaty relief is denied to an interposed company whose owners would not get it directly and which has no real business of its own (§ 50d(3) EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF136, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:136 | The German company withholds 25% plus 5.5% surcharge, 26.375% in all, whoever the shareholder is. For a non-resident shareholder without a German business, that settles the German tax. The excess over the treaty rate is reclaimed from the BZSt under § 50c(3). Example: a treaty limit of 15% gives a refund of 26.375% − 15% = 11.375% of the gross dividend. A foreign company that cannot use a treaty still gets two fifths of the tax back (§ 44a(9)). The treaty rate itself depends on the treaty; this skill has no rate table by country. | Full dividend-withholding/refund paragraph retained at current LF140; historical absent-country-table warning replaced by actual de-treaty-withholding-rates referral. |
| fb85812a814d:140 | Which rule applies depends on the income: | Exact wording retained at current LF144, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:142 | - **Capital income under the flat tax (§ 32d(1)), such as foreign dividends and interest of a private investor.** § 34c does not apply (§ 34c(1) sentence 1, second half). The foreign tax is credited under § 32d(5): at most 25% of each item, and at most the German tax on the capital income. The flat tax is then (e − 4q) / 4 without church tax, where e is the capital income after the Sparer-Pauschbetrag and q the creditable foreign tax (§ 32d(1) sentence 4). Income that had no German Kapitalertragsteuer, for example through a foreign broker, is declared in the return (§ 32d(3)). | Exact wording retained at current LF146, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:143 | - **Other foreign income taxed at the tariff (for example foreign rent, foreign business income, or capital income taxed at the tariff after the § 32d(6) option).** § 34c(1) applies where no treaty exists; where a treaty provides for a credit, § 34c(6) applies the same method. The foreign tax is credited up to the German tax on that income: | DE-L11 corrected own32d(6) credit routing/refund-first illustration, current foreign-credit section and Example2; original mistaken34c inclusion/refundable2250 illustration not retained as correct. |
| fb85812a814d:145 |   credit cap = German tariff tax × foreign income from that country / taxable income (sentence 2: the average rate on the whole taxable income, applied to the foreign income). | Exact wording retained at current LF149, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:147 |   Foreign tax above the cap is lost. Only tax that was set, paid and reduced by any refund due abroad counts. Instead of the credit the taxpayer can deduct the foreign tax from the income (§ 34c(2)). | Exact wording retained at current LF151, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:148 | - **Income a treaty exempts in Germany** (often foreign employment income or foreign rent): no credit, but it raises the rate under § 32b(1) no. 3. Exception: rent from property in another EU/EEA state, and profits of an EU/EEA permanent establishment that fails the active-business test of § 2a(2), do not raise the rate (§ 32b(1) sentence 2). | Exact wording retained at current LF152, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:150 | What counts as foreign income is set out in § 34d EStG. | Exact wording retained at current LF154, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| fb85812a814d:154 | \| Step \| Rule \| | Exact wording retained at current LF158, Deadlines and filing |
| fb85812a814d:156 | \| Who files \| A non-resident with German income not settled by withholding (rent, a German business, sale of German land), or who applies for an assessment (§ 1(3), refunds of wage tax, § 50a net assessment). Return under § 25(3) EStG \| | Exact wording retained at current LF160, Deadlines and filing |
| fb85812a814d:157 | \| Competent tax office \| For a non-resident: the office where the German assets are, or the most valuable part; failing that, where the activity is mainly carried on (§ 19(2) AO). Employees under § 1(3) or § 50(2) no. 4: the employer's Betriebsstättenfinanzamt (§ 46(2) nos. 7, 9; § 50(2) sentences 3 to 6 EStG). § 50a net assessments: the BZSt (§ 50(2) sentence 9) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF161, Deadlines and filing |
| fb85812a814d:158 | \| Pensions from Germany \| A non-resident whose only German income is pensions under § 49(1) nos. 7 and 10: Finanzamt Neubrandenburg, also where such a person applies under § 1(3) (§ 19(6) AO; § 1 EStZustV) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF162, Deadlines and filing |
| fb85812a814d:159 | \| Own return, 2026 \| 31 July 2027, a Saturday, so Monday 2 August 2027 (§ 149(2), § 108(3) AO) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF163, Deadlines and filing |
| fb85812a814d:160 | \| With a tax adviser, 2026 \| 29 February 2028 (§ 149(3) AO) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF164, Deadlines and filing |
| fb85812a814d:161 | \| § 50a withholding return (payer) \| By the 10th of the month after each calendar quarter, to the BZSt (§ 50a(5)) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF165, Deadlines and filing |
| fb85812a814d:162 | \| § 50c refund \| Four years from the end of the year the payment was received (§ 50c(3)) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF166, Deadlines and filing |
| fb85812a814d:163 | \| Electronic filing \| Compulsory where there is business or self-employed income (§ 25(4) EStG) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF167, Deadlines and filing |
| fb85812a814d:167 | - **Late filing surcharge (§ 152 AO).** 0.25% per started month of the tax less prepayments and withholding, at least EUR 25 per month, at most EUR 25,000. It is compulsory if the return is not filed within 14 months after the end of the year (for 2026: by 29 February 2028), unless an extension was granted, the tax is nil or negative, or the tax does not exceed prepayments and withholding (§ 152(2), (3) AO). | Exact wording retained at current LF171, Penalties and interest |
| fb85812a814d:168 | - **Interest (§ 233a, § 238 AO).** 0.15% per full month (1.8% a year) on the balance, from 15 months after year end: from 1 April 2028 for 2026. | Exact wording retained at current LF172, Penalties and interest |
| fb85812a814d:169 | - **Late payment (§ 240 AO).** 1% per started month on the overdue tax rounded down to EUR 50. | Exact wording retained at current LF173, Penalties and interest |
| fb85812a814d:170 | - **§ 50a payer.** The payer is liable for tax it did not withhold or pay over (§ 50a(5) sentence 5). The creditor can also be pursued. | Exact wording retained at current LF174, Penalties and interest |
| fb85812a814d:174 | - **Treaties override.** A treaty can take away Germany's right to tax (for example employment income under the 183-day rule) or cap it (dividends, royalties). Withholding still happens first; relief is by exemption certificate or refund (§ 50c). | Exact wording retained at current LF178, Cross-border points |
| fb85812a814d:175 | - **Moving in or out during the year.** A person who is fully liable for part of the year is assessed once; foreign income of the non-resident months raises the rate (§ 2(7), § 32b(1) no. 2 EStG). Not set out further here. | Exact wording retained at current LF179, Cross-border points |
| fb85812a814d:176 | - **Leaving with shares.** See leaving-germany-exit-tax, including extended limited liability under § 2 AStG after a move to a low-tax country. | Exact wording retained at current LF180, Cross-border points |
| fb85812a814d:177 | - **VAT.** A non-resident letting German property or performing in Germany may also have German VAT duties. Not covered here; see germany-vat-return. | Exact wording retained at current LF181, Cross-border points |
| fb85812a814d:178 | - **Church tax.** Not dealt with here; it is set by state law. | Exact wording retained at current LF182, Cross-border points |
| fb85812a814d:184 | Anna lives in Vienna and has no home in Germany. Her German flat produces a rental result of EUR 24,000 for 2026, computed as in de-rental-income. Her only other income is a rental result of EUR 2,000 from a garage she lets in Vienna. (Invented case.) | Exact wording retained at current LF188, Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat |
| fb85812a814d:186 | **Limited liability (default).** No personal deductions, no EUR 36 lump sum. Taxable income EUR 24,000. The tariff is applied to 24,000 + 12,348 = 36,348. z = (36,348 − 17,799) / 10,000 = 1.8549. Tax = (173.10 × 1.8549 + 2,397) × 1.8549 + 1,034.87 = 6,076.65, rounded down EUR 6,076. Below EUR 20,350, so no surcharge. | Exact wording retained at current LF190, Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat |
| fb85812a814d:188 | **§ 1(3) option.** German income is 24,000 / 26,000 = 92.3% of her total, and the Austrian income is under EUR 12,348 (Austria is in country group 1), so both tests pass. She needs a certificate from the Austrian tax office for the EUR 2,000. Taxable income 24,000 − 36 = EUR 23,964. The Austrian rent is not subject to German tax, so it raises the rate (§ 32b(1) no. 5; the EU/EEA rent exception in § 32b(1) sentence 2 covers only no. 3): tax on 23,964 + 2,000 = 25,964 is EUR 3,107, a rate of 11.9666%. Tax = 23,964 × 11.9666% = EUR 2,867. The option saves EUR 3,209. | Exact wording retained at current LF192, Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat |
| fb85812a814d:190 | The tax office rounds the progression rate; the example keeps it unrounded, which can move the result by one euro. If Anna also had large Austrian income (say a salary of EUR 40,000), she would fail both tests and stay on the limited-liability figure. | Exact wording retained at current LF194, Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat |

| fb85812a814d:222 | Jan lives in Munich. In 2026 he receives EUR 10,000 of dividends from a foreign company through a foreign broker. The source country withheld 15% (EUR 1,500), its treaty rate. He has no other capital income and is not a church member. (Invented case.) | Exact wording retained at current LF226, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| fb85812a814d:224 | **Which regime.** These are private capital income under § 32d(1) EStG, so § 34c does not apply (§ 34c(1) sentence 1, second half, and § 34c(6) sentence 2). The credit is under § 32d(5). Because no German bank withheld, he declares the dividends in the return (§ 32d(3)). | Exact wording retained at current LF228, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| fb85812a814d:226 | - Capital income after the Sparer-Pauschbetrag of EUR 1,000 (see de-capital-gains): e = EUR 9,000. | Exact wording retained at current LF230, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| fb85812a814d:227 | - German tax before credit: 25% × 9,000 = EUR 2,250. | Exact wording retained at current LF231, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| fb85812a814d:228 | - Cap 1: 25% of the dividend, EUR 2,500. Cap 2: the German tax on the capital income, EUR 2,250. The EUR 1,500 paid is under both, so q = EUR 1,500 is credited in full. | Exact wording retained at current LF232, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| fb85812a814d:229 | - Flat tax: (9,000 − 4 × 1,500) / 4 = EUR 750. | Exact wording retained at current LF233, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| fb85812a814d:230 | - Solidarity surcharge: 5.5% × 750 = EUR 41.25. The EUR 20,350 threshold does not apply to § 32d(3) tax. | Exact wording retained at current LF234, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| fb85812a814d:231 | - Total German tax on the dividends: EUR 791.25. | Exact wording retained at current LF235, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| fb85812a814d:233 | If he had chosen the tariff under § 32d(6) (only worth it on a low income), § 32d(5) still governs the credit, applied to the extra tariff tax on the capital income. If the foreign rate were 30% (EUR 3,000), only EUR 2,250 could be credited. The part above the treaty rate is reclaimed from the source country under its treaty, not from Germany; the rest is lost. | DE-L11 corrected own32d(6) credit routing/refund-first illustration, current foreign-credit section and Example2; original mistaken34c inclusion/refundable2250 illustration not retained as correct. |

| fb85812a814d:262 | 1. Where do you live, and do you keep any home in Germany you can use? Did you stay in Germany more than six months in a row? This decides full or limited liability. | Exact wording retained at current LF266, Ask the client first |
| fb85812a814d:263 | 2. What German income do you have (wages for work done in Germany, rent, a business, director or board fees, performances, royalties, dividends), and was tax withheld from it? | Exact wording retained at current LF267, Ask the client first |
| fb85812a814d:264 | 3. What is your total income from all countries for 2026? This decides the § 1(3) test. | Exact wording retained at current LF268, Ask the client first |
| fb85812a814d:265 | 4. Your nationality, and where your spouse lives. EU/EEA nationals may get splitting under § 1a. | Exact wording retained at current LF269, Ask the client first |
| fb85812a814d:266 | 5. Which country taxes you as a resident, and do you have its residence certificate? Treaty refunds and the § 1(3) option need one. | Exact wording retained at current LF270, Ask the client first |
| fb85812a814d:267 | 6. For a resident with foreign income: what type of income, which country, how much tax was paid, and was any of it refundable there? | Exact wording retained at current LF271, Ask the client first |

| 62b7639a5a68:18 | This skill decides whether a person is taxed in Germany on worldwide income or only on German income, then works out how the German income is taxed: by assessment, by wage tax, or by a flat withholding. It also covers the reverse case: a German resident with foreign income who wants credit for the foreign tax. | Exact wording retained at current LF18, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:20 | See also, and do not repeat here: | Exact wording retained at current LF20, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:22 | - **de-employee-income-tax**: the 2026 tariff, Werbungskosten and special expenses for a resident employee. The tariff formula there is the one used below. | Exact wording retained at current LF22, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:23 | - **de-rental-income**: how to compute the rental result (AfA, Werbungskosten, Anlage V). A non-resident landlord computes it the same way. | Exact wording retained at current LF23, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:24 | - **de-capital-gains**: the flat tax on investment income, the Sparer-Pauschbetrag and the loss pots. | Exact wording retained at current LF24, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:25 | - **de-payroll** and **germany-payroll**: running wage tax for an employee, including tax class I. | Exact wording retained at current LF25, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:26 | - **de-corporate-tax**: corporate tax at 15%, also for the German permanent establishment of a foreign company. | Exact wording retained at current LF26, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:27 | - **leaving-germany-exit-tax**: shareholders who move out, and extended limited liability after a move to a low-tax country (§ 2 AStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF27, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:28 | - **germany-to-switzerland-cross-border-tax**: Swiss commuters and moves to Switzerland. | Exact wording retained at current LF28, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:29 | - **de-treaty-withholding-rates**: treaty withholding rates by country, § 43b, § 50g and § 50d(3). | Exact wording retained at current LF29, Germany: non-residents with German income, and foreign tax credits, tax year 2026 |
| 62b7639a5a68:33 | **Unlimited liability (§ 1(1) EStG).** A person with a residence (Wohnsitz) or habitual abode (gewöhnlicher Aufenthalt) in Germany is taxed on worldwide income. | Exact wording retained at current LF33, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:35 | - **Residence (§ 8 AO):** a home the person keeps and uses, in circumstances showing they will keep and use it. A flat kept available in Germany is enough, even if the person spends most of the year abroad. | Exact wording retained at current LF35, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:36 | - **Habitual abode (§ 9 AO):** staying in a way that shows the stay is not just temporary. A continuous stay of more than six months always counts, from the first day; short breaks are ignored. The six-month rule does not apply to a purely private stay (visit, holiday, cure) of up to one year (§ 9 sentence 3 AO). | Exact wording retained at current LF36, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:37 | - **German state employees abroad (§ 1(2) EStG):** German nationals paid from a German public purse, taxed abroad only like non-residents, stay fully liable. | Exact wording retained at current LF37, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:39 | **Limited liability (§ 1(4) EStG).** A person with neither residence nor habitual abode in Germany is taxed only on German-source income listed in § 49 EStG. | Exact wording retained at current LF39, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:41 | **The § 1(3) option.** A non-resident can apply to be treated as fully liable for their German income if, in the calendar year, either: | Exact wording retained at current LF41, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:43 | - at least 90% of their total income is subject to German income tax, or | Exact wording retained at current LF43, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:44 | - the income not subject to German tax is no more than the Grundfreibetrag, EUR 12,348 for 2026. The amount is cut to ¾, ½ or ¼ for residents of lower-cost countries (BMF country groups; Austria and most of Western Europe are in group 1, full amount). | Exact wording retained at current LF44, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:46 | German income that a tax treaty limits to a reduced rate (for example dividends) counts as not subject to German tax for the test (§ 1(3) sentence 3). The foreign income must be shown by a certificate from the foreign tax authority (§ 1(3) sentence 5). § 50a withholding still applies (§ 1(3) sentence 6). | Exact wording retained at current LF46, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:48 | **§ 1a EStG, EU/EEA nationals.** An EU or EEA national who is fully liable under § 1(1) or treated so under § 1(3) can have a spouse who lives in another EU/EEA state treated as fully liable, so the couple gets joint assessment and splitting. For the § 1(3) test the income of both spouses counts and the Grundfreibetrag is doubled to EUR 24,696 (§ 1a(1) no. 2). A non-EU/EEA national under § 1(3) gets the Grundfreibetrag but not splitting. | Exact wording retained at current LF48, Who this applies to |
| 62b7639a5a68:50 | **Companies.** A company with neither its seat nor its place of management in Germany has limited corporate tax liability on its German income (§ 2 no. 1 KStG). Income settled by withholding is final unless it belongs to a German business (§ 32(1) KStG). A German permanent establishment is assessed at the 15% corporate rate (§ 23 KStG; see de-corporate-tax). | Exact wording retained at current LF50, Who this applies to |

(§ 50(2) EStG) |

| 62b7639a5a68:108 | 1. **Income.** Compute each type as for a resident. Business expenses and Werbungskosten count only if they relate to the German income. | Exact wording retained at current LF108, Step 3: the assessment of a non-resident (§ 50(1) EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:109 | 2. **No personal deductions.** Special expenses, the EUR 36 lump sum, single-parent relief, child allowances, extraordinary burdens and § 35a credits do not apply (§ 50(1) sentence 4). There is no splitting. | DE-L13 corrected general exclusion with own50(1a) professional-pension exception at current LF109. |
| 62b7639a5a68:110 | 3. **Employees are the exception.** For employment income under § 49(1) no. 4, pension and health insurance contributions and the EUR 36 lump sum are allowed, for the period of the German employment and up to that employment income (§ 50(1) sentence 5). The employee lump sum (EUR 1,230, § 9a) is a Werbungskosten lump sum and applies anyway; it and the EUR 36 are cut pro rata for part-year work (§ 50(1) sentence 6). | Exact wording retained at current LF110, Step 3: the assessment of a non-resident (§ 50(1) EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:111 | 4. **Grundfreibetrag added back.** Apply the tariff to taxable income plus EUR 12,348. For employment income, add only the part of EUR 12,348 above the employment income less allowed deductions. In effect a non-resident employee keeps the allowance on wages; a landlord or business owner does not. | Exact wording retained at current LF111, Step 3: the assessment of a non-resident (§ 50(1) EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:112 | 5. **Solidarity surcharge** at 5.5% of the income tax, with the same EUR 20,350 threshold as residents. | Exact wording retained at current LF112, Step 3: the assessment of a non-resident (§ 50(1) EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:116 | The person is taxed as a resident on their German income: Grundfreibetrag, special expenses, the EUR 36 lump sum and the resident deductions apply. Income that is not subject to German tax is left out of the base but raises the rate (progression, § 32b(1) no. 5 EStG): the tax rate is found on taxable income plus that foreign income, then applied to taxable income alone. Splitting needs § 1a. Foreign capital income is a special case: § 2(5b) EStG keeps capital income under § 32d(1) out of "income" wherever the law refers to it, and whether foreign interest or dividends of a § 1(3) applicant count in the 90% test and in the progression is not settled here (see Not yet confirmed). | Exact wording retained at current LF116, Step 4: the § 1(3) assessment instead |
| 62b7639a5a68:120 | - Non-resident employees are in tax class I (§ 38b(1) no. 1(b) EStG), unless treated as fully liable under § 1(3) or § 1a. | Exact wording retained at current LF120, Wage tax for non-resident employees |
| 62b7639a5a68:121 | - The employer's Betriebsstättenfinanzamt (the tax office for the place of business), not a residence office, sets and changes their wage tax data and issues the tax ID (§ 39(2) sentence 2 and § 39(3) EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF121, Wage tax for non-resident employees |
| 62b7639a5a68:122 | - A § 1(3) or § 1a employee with wage tax data set this way must file (§ 46(2) no. 7(b) EStG). An employee who wants § 1(3) treatment only in the assessment applies with a return (§ 46(2) no. 9), at the employer's Betriebsstättenfinanzamt. | Exact wording retained at current LF122, Wage tax for non-resident employees |
| 62b7639a5a68:126 | - The German payer withholds when the payment is made: 15% of gross, 30% for supervisory board fees, plus 5.5% surcharge on the tax. Travel and hotel costs the payer refunds count as receipts only above actual cost and the per diem lump sums. | Exact wording retained at current LF126, § 50a withholding (artists, athletes, royalties, supervisory boards) |
| 62b7639a5a68:127 | - For an EU/EEA creditor the payer may deduct costs the creditor proves, then withhold 30% (individual) or 15% (company) of the net (§ 50a(3)). | DE-L12 corrected statutory net-route scope, current LF64/127; royalties no3 excluded, actual eligible nationals/residence/company and proven direct-cost conditions retained. |
| 62b7639a5a68:128 | - The payer files the withholding return with the BZSt by the 10th of the month after each calendar quarter and pays it over (§ 50a(5)). It must file even when nothing is withheld. The payer is liable for tax not withheld; the creditor can also be pursued. | Exact wording retained at current LF128, § 50a withholding (artists, athletes, royalties, supervisory boards) |
| 62b7639a5a68:129 | - On request the payer issues a certificate of the tax withheld (§ 50a(5) sentence 7). The creditor needs it for a refund. | Exact wording retained at current LF129, § 50a withholding (artists, athletes, royalties, supervisory boards) |
| 62b7639a5a68:133 | - The payer withholds in full even when a treaty limits German tax, unless the creditor holds an exemption certificate (Freistellungsbescheinigung) from the BZSt (§ 50c(1), (2)). For dividends, a treaty exemption certificate is given only to a company holding at least 10% of a German company. | Exact wording retained at current LF133, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:134 | - Small royalties: where a treaty bars German tax and the year's royalties from that payer stay at or below EUR 10,000, the payer need not withhold (§ 50c(2) no. 2), but must still file the withholding return. | Exact wording retained at current LF134, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:135 | - Refund: the creditor applies to the BZSt, electronically, within four years from the end of the year of receipt, with a residence certificate from their own tax authority and the payer's tax certificate (§ 50c(3), (5)). The BZSt warns that refunds can take more than 20 months. | Exact wording retained at current LF135, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:136 | - Companies: treaty relief is denied to an interposed company whose owners would not get it directly and which has no real business of its own (§ 50d(3) EStG). | Exact wording retained at current LF136, Treaty relief: exemption and refund (§ 50c EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:140 | The German company withholds 25% plus 5.5% surcharge, 26.375% in all, whoever the shareholder is. For a non-resident shareholder without a German business, that settles the German tax. The excess over the treaty rate is reclaimed from the BZSt under § 50c(3). Example: a treaty limit of 15% gives a refund of 26.375% − 15% = 11.375% of the gross dividend. A foreign company that cannot use a treaty still gets two fifths of the tax back (§ 44a(9)). The treaty rate itself depends on the treaty; see de-treaty-withholding-rates for the rates by country. | Exact wording retained at current LF140, Kapitalertragsteuer on dividends to non-residents |
| 62b7639a5a68:144 | Which rule applies depends on the income: | Exact wording retained at current LF144, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:146 | - **Capital income under the flat tax (§ 32d(1)), such as foreign dividends and interest of a private investor.** § 34c does not apply (§ 34c(1) sentence 1, second half). The foreign tax is credited under § 32d(5): at most 25% of each item, and at most the German tax on the capital income. The flat tax is then (e − 4q) / 4 without church tax, where e is the capital income after the Sparer-Pauschbetrag and q the creditable foreign tax (§ 32d(1) sentence 4). Income that had no German Kapitalertragsteuer, for example through a foreign broker, is declared in the return (§ 32d(3)). | Exact wording retained at current LF146, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:147 | - **Other foreign income taxed at the tariff (for example foreign rent, foreign business income, or capital income taxed at the tariff after the § 32d(6) option).** § 34c(1) applies where no treaty exists; where a treaty provides for a credit, § 34c(6) applies the same method. The foreign tax is credited up to the German tax on that income: | DE-L11 corrected own32d(6) credit routing/refund-first illustration, current foreign-credit section and Example2; original mistaken34c inclusion/refundable2250 illustration not retained as correct. |
| 62b7639a5a68:149 |   credit cap = German tariff tax × foreign income from that country / taxable income (sentence 2: the average rate on the whole taxable income, applied to the foreign income). | Exact wording retained at current LF149, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:151 |   Foreign tax above the cap is lost. Only tax that was set, paid and reduced by any refund due abroad counts. Instead of the credit the taxpayer can deduct the foreign tax from the income (§ 34c(2)). | Exact wording retained at current LF151, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:152 | - **Income a treaty exempts in Germany** (often foreign employment income or foreign rent): no credit, but it raises the rate under § 32b(1) no. 3. Exception: rent from property in another EU/EEA state, and profits of an EU/EEA permanent establishment that fails the active-business test of § 2a(2), do not raise the rate (§ 32b(1) sentence 2). | Exact wording retained at current LF152, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:154 | What counts as foreign income is set out in § 34d EStG. | Exact wording retained at current LF154, Foreign tax credit for residents (§ 34c and § 32d(5) EStG) |
| 62b7639a5a68:158 | \| Step \| Rule \| | Exact wording retained at current LF158, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:160 | \| Who files \| A non-resident with German income not settled by withholding (rent, a German business, sale of German land), or who applies for an assessment (§ 1(3), refunds of wage tax, § 50a net assessment). Return under § 25(3) EStG \| | Exact wording retained at current LF160, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:161 | \| Competent tax office \| For a non-resident: the office where the German assets are, or the most valuable part; failing that, where the activity is mainly carried on (§ 19(2) AO). Employees under § 1(3) or § 50(2) no. 4: the employer's Betriebsstättenfinanzamt (§ 46(2) nos. 7, 9; § 50(2) sentences 3 to 6 EStG). § 50a net assessments: the BZSt (§ 50(2) sentence 9) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF161, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:162 | \| Pensions from Germany \| A non-resident whose only German income is pensions under § 49(1) nos. 7 and 10: Finanzamt Neubrandenburg, also where such a person applies under § 1(3) (§ 19(6) AO; § 1 EStZustV) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF162, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:163 | \| Own return, 2026 \| 31 July 2027, a Saturday, so Monday 2 August 2027 (§ 149(2), § 108(3) AO) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF163, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:164 | \| With a tax adviser, 2026 \| 29 February 2028 (§ 149(3) AO) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF164, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:165 | \| § 50a withholding return (payer) \| By the 10th of the month after each calendar quarter, to the BZSt (§ 50a(5)) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF165, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:166 | \| § 50c refund \| Four years from the end of the year the payment was received (§ 50c(3)) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF166, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:167 | \| Electronic filing \| Compulsory where there is business or self-employed income (§ 25(4) EStG) \| | Exact wording retained at current LF167, Deadlines and filing |
| 62b7639a5a68:171 | - **Late filing surcharge (§ 152 AO).** 0.25% per started month of the tax less prepayments and withholding, at least EUR 25 per month, at most EUR 25,000. It is compulsory if the return is not filed within 14 months after the end of the year (for 2026: by 29 February 2028), unless an extension was granted, the tax is nil or negative, or the tax does not exceed prepayments and withholding (§ 152(2), (3) AO). | Exact wording retained at current LF171, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:172 | - **Interest (§ 233a, § 238 AO).** 0.15% per full month (1.8% a year) on the balance, from 15 months after year end: from 1 April 2028 for 2026. | Exact wording retained at current LF172, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:173 | - **Late payment (§ 240 AO).** 1% per started month on the overdue tax rounded down to EUR 50. | Exact wording retained at current LF173, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:174 | - **§ 50a payer.** The payer is liable for tax it did not withhold or pay over (§ 50a(5) sentence 5). The creditor can also be pursued. | Exact wording retained at current LF174, Penalties and interest |
| 62b7639a5a68:178 | - **Treaties override.** A treaty can take away Germany's right to tax (for example employment income under the 183-day rule) or cap it (dividends, royalties). Withholding still happens first; relief is by exemption certificate or refund (§ 50c). | Exact wording retained at current LF178, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:179 | - **Moving in or out during the year.** A person who is fully liable for part of the year is assessed once; foreign income of the non-resident months raises the rate (§ 2(7), § 32b(1) no. 2 EStG). Not set out further here. | Exact wording retained at current LF179, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:180 | - **Leaving with shares.** See leaving-germany-exit-tax, including extended limited liability under § 2 AStG after a move to a low-tax country. | Exact wording retained at current LF180, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:181 | - **VAT.** A non-resident letting German property or performing in Germany may also have German VAT duties. Not covered here; see germany-vat-return. | Exact wording retained at current LF181, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:182 | - **Church tax.** Not dealt with here; it is set by state law. | Exact wording retained at current LF182, Cross-border points |
| 62b7639a5a68:188 | Anna lives in Vienna and has no home in Germany. Her German flat produces a rental result of EUR 24,000 for 2026, computed as in de-rental-income. Her only other income is a rental result of EUR 2,000 from a garage she lets in Vienna. (Invented case.) | Exact wording retained at current LF188, Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat |
| 62b7639a5a68:190 | **Limited liability (default).** No personal deductions, no EUR 36 lump sum. Taxable income EUR 24,000. The tariff is applied to 24,000 + 12,348 = 36,348. z = (36,348 − 17,799) / 10,000 = 1.8549. Tax = (173.10 × 1.8549 + 2,397) × 1.8549 + 1,034.87 = 6,076.65, rounded down EUR 6,076. Below EUR 20,350, so no surcharge. | Exact wording retained at current LF190, Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat |
| 62b7639a5a68:192 | **§ 1(3) option.** German income is 24,000 / 26,000 = 92.3% of her total, and the Austrian income is under EUR 12,348 (Austria is in country group 1), so both tests pass. She needs a certificate from the Austrian tax office for the EUR 2,000. Taxable income 24,000 − 36 = EUR 23,964. The Austrian rent is not subject to German tax, so it raises the rate (§ 32b(1) no. 5; the EU/EEA rent exception in § 32b(1) sentence 2 covers only no. 3): tax on 23,964 + 2,000 = 25,964 is EUR 3,107, a rate of 11.9666%. Tax = 23,964 × 11.9666% = EUR 2,867. The option saves EUR 3,209. | Exact wording retained at current LF192, Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat |
| 62b7639a5a68:194 | The tax office rounds the progression rate; the example keeps it unrounded, which can move the result by one euro. If Anna also had large Austrian income (say a salary of EUR 40,000), she would fail both tests and stay on the limited-liability figure. | Exact wording retained at current LF194, Example 1: Austrian resident with a German rental flat |

| 62b7639a5a68:226 | Jan lives in Munich. In 2026 he receives EUR 10,000 of dividends from a foreign company through a foreign broker. The source country withheld 15% (EUR 1,500), its treaty rate. He has no other capital income and is not a church member. (Invented case.) | Exact wording retained at current LF226, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| 62b7639a5a68:228 | **Which regime.** These are private capital income under § 32d(1) EStG, so § 34c does not apply (§ 34c(1) sentence 1, second half, and § 34c(6) sentence 2). The credit is under § 32d(5). | 62b7639a5a68:230 | - Capital income after the Sparer-Pauschbetrag of EUR 1,000 (see de-capital-gains): e = EUR 9,000. | Exact wording retained at current LF230, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| 62b7639a5a68:231 | - German tax before credit: 25% × 9,000 = EUR 2,250. | Exact wording retained at current LF231, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| 62b7639a5a68:232 | - Cap 1: 25% of the dividend, EUR 2,500. Cap 2: the German tax on the capital income, EUR 2,250. | 62b7639a5a68:233 | - Flat tax: (9,000 − 4 × 1,500) / 4 = EUR 750. | Exact wording retained at current LF233, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |

| 62b7639a5a68:235 | - Total German tax on the dividends: EUR 791.25. | Exact wording retained at current LF235, Example 2: German resident with foreign dividends taxed 15% abroad |
| 62b7639a5a68:237 | If he had chosen the tariff under § 32d(6) (only worth it on a low income), § 32d(5) still governs the credit, applied to the extra tariff tax on the capital income. | 62b7639a5a68:266 | 1. Where do you live, and do you keep any home in Germany you can use? Did you stay in Germany more than six months in a row? This decides full or limited liability. | Exact wording retained at current LF266, Ask the client first |
